Hardcoding Calculated Values\n```python\n# Bad: Calculating in Python and hardcoding result\ntotal = df['Sales'].sum()\nsheet['B10'] = total  # Hardcodes 5000\n\n# Bad: Computing growth rate in Python\ngrowth = (df.iloc[-1]['Revenue'] - df.iloc[0]['Revenue']) / df.iloc[0]['Revenue']\nsheet['C5'] = growth  # Hardcodes 0.15\n\n# Bad: Python calculation for average\navg = sum(values) / len(values)\nsheet['D20'] = avg  # Hardcodes 42.5\n```\n\n### ✅ CORRECT - Using Excel Formulas\n```python\n# Good: Let Excel calculate the sum\nsheet['B10'] = '=SUM(B2:B9)'\n\n# Good: Growth rate as Excel formula\nsheet['C5'] = '=(C4-C2)/C2'\n\n# Good: Average using Excel function\nsheet['D20'] = '=AVERAGE(D2:D19)'\n```\n\nThis applies to ALL calculations - totals, percentages, ratios, differences, etc. The spreadsheet should be able to recalculate when source data changes.\n\n## Common Workflow\n1. **Choose tool**: pandas for data, openpyxl for formulas/formatting\n2. **Create/Load**: Create new workbook or load existing file\n3. **Modify**: Add/edit data, formulas, and formatting\n4. **Save**: Write to file\n5. **Recalculate formulas (MANDATORY IF USING FORMULAS)**: Use the recalc.py script\n   ```bash\n   python recalc.py output.xlsx\n   ```\n6. **Verify and fix any errors**:\n   - Use the provided `recalc.py` script to recalculate formulas:\n\n```bash\npython recalc.py <excel_file> [timeout_seconds]\n```\n\nExample:\n```bash\npython recalc.py output.xlsx 30\n```\n\nThe script:\n- Automatically sets up LibreOffice macro on first run\n- Recalculates all formulas in all sheets\n- Scans ALL cells for Excel errors (#REF!, #DIV/0!, etc.)\n- Returns JSON with detailed sample references**: Verify they pull correct values before building full model\n- [ ] **Column mapping**: Confirm Excel columns match (e.g., column 64 = BL, not BK)\n- [ ] **Row offset**: Remember Excel rows are 1-indexed (DataFrame row 5 = Excel row 6)\n\n### Common Pitfalls\n- [ ] **NaN handling**: Check for null values with `pd.notna()`\n- [ ] **Far-right columns**: FY data often in columns 50+\n- [ ] **Multiple matches**: Search all occurrences, not just first\n- [ ] **Division by zero**: Check denominators before using `/` in formulas (#DIV/0!)\n- [ ] **Wrong references**: Verify all values\n\n### Interpreting recalc.py Output\nThe script returns JSON with error details:\n```json\n{\n  \"status\": \"success\",           // or \"errors_found\"\n  \"total_errors\": 0, Column D: LnK = natural log of capital stock\n2. Column E: LnY = natural log of real GDP\n3. Column F: LnZ = log(Y/K) = productivity residual\n4. Column G: LnZ_HP = YELLOW HIGHLIGHTED - to be optimized\n5. Column P5: Objective function = minimize SSE of residuals + smoothness\n\nUsing Excel's Solver:\n→ Minimize: P5 (sum of squared deviations + lambda * 2nd differences)\n→ By changing: G6:G27 (LnZ_HP values)\n→ Lambda = 1600 (standard for annual data)\n\nResult: Smoothed trend of productivity (LnZ_trend = Column K)\n\nPhase 3: Production Function Extension\n───────────────────────────────────────\nUsing Cobb-Douglas: Y = A * K^α * L^(1-α)\n\nWhere:\n• Y = Real GDP\n• K = Capital stock (extended with K/Y anchor)\n• A = Total Factor Productivity (from HP filter)\n• α = Capital elasticity = 0.3 (developing economy)\n\nThe Production_Function sheet:\n• Calculates K/Y ratios from 2002-2023 (shows 2.4-2.6 typical)\n• Extends K to 2041 using average K/Y of recent 9 years\n• Links LnZ_trend from HP Filter (extends via TREND formula)\n• Computes Ystar_base = potential output without investment\n• Computes Ystar_with = potential output with investment shock\n\nPhase 4: Investment Shock Scenario\n───────────────────────────────────\nInvestment parameters:\n• Duration: 8 years (2026-2033)\n• Total amount: $6.5 billion\n• Annual flow: $812.5 million/year\n• Depreciation: 8.5% per year (calculated from CFC data)\n\nCapital accumulation:\nK(t) = K(t-1) × (1 - δ) + Investment(t)\nwhere δ = depreciation rate\n\nOutput impact:\n• Ystar_base = potential output without investment\n• Ystar_with = potential output with accumulated extra capital\n• Impact = Ystar_with - Ystar_base\n\nResults appear in:\n• Investment sheet: Year-by-year capital and output\n• Summary sheet: Cumulative annual impacts (2026-2049)\n\n================================================================================\nSTEP-BY-STEP INSTRUCTIONS\n================================================================================\n\n1. COLLECT DATA (1-2 hours)\n   ─────────────────────────\n   □ Download PWT 11.0 → Fill PWT sheet column B\n   □ Download ECB data → Fill CFC data sheet column B (2015-2023)\n   □ Verify WEO data in WEO_Data sheet (already 90% complete)\n\n2. CALCULATE DEPRECIATION RATE (automatic)\n   ────────────────────────────────────────\n   □ Once CFC data is filled, Production!B3 auto-calculates\n   Formula: =AVERAGE('CFC data'!D6:D13)\n   Result: Average depreciation rate of 2016-2023 (8 years)\n\n3. RUN HP FILTER (30 minutes)\n   ──────────────────────────\n   In Excel, go to Data → Solver:\n   \n   Set Objective Cell: Production!P5\n   To: Minimum\n   By Changing Cells: Production!$G$6:$G$27\n   \n   Click Solve → It will optimize the smoothed trend line\n   \n   Verify: Column N (Sanity Check) should show \"OK\" if solved properly\n\n4. EXTEND TREND (automatic)\n   ────────────────────────\n   □ TREND formula automatically extends LnZ_trend to 2041\n   □ No manual action needed\n   Location: Production_Function!E24:E40\n\n5. CALCULATE PRODUCTION FUNCTION (automatic)\n   ──────────────────────────────────────────\n   □ Column C: K extended using K/Y anchor (formula-driven)\n   □ Column G: Ystar_base = exp(LnZ_trend) × K^0.3\n   □ Column J: K_with = K + cumulative investment\n   □ Column K: Ystar_with = output with investment shock\n   □ Column L: Impact = Ystar_with - Ystar_base\n\n6. VIEW RESULTS\n   ────────────\n   □ Summary sheet: Annual GDP impacts (2026-2049)\n   □ Total output gain from $6.5B investment over 8 years\n   □ Year-by-year decomposition available in Production_Function\n\n================================================================================\nKEY ASSUMPTIONS & PARAMETERS\n================================================================================\n\nEconomic Parameters:\n├─ Capital elasticity (α): 0.3 (typical for developing economies)\n├─ Long-term GDP growth: 7.2% (IMF WEO 2027+ projection)\n├─ Depreciation rate: CALCULATED from ECB data (typically 4-6% for dev. economies)\n├─ HP Filter Lambda: 1600 (standard for annual data)\n└─ Investment duration: 8 years (2026-2033)\n\nData Sources:\n├─ Real GDP levels: Worldometer + IMF WEO (2000-2025)\n├─ Growth rates: IMF WEO October 2025\n├─ Capital stock: Penn World Table 11.0 (2023)\n├─ Depreciation: ECB statistical database (2015-2023)\n└─ Labor data: Implicit in TFP calculations\n\n================================================================================\nEXPECTED RESULTS\n================================================================================\n\nBased on typical developing economies:\n• Annual GDP impact: $400-800 million per year (during investment period)\n• Cumulative impact (8 years): $3.2-6.4 billion\n• Long-term impact: Declining as cumulative capital depreciates\n• Elasticity: ~0.3-0.4% increase in output per 1% increase in capital\n\nThe actual results will depend on:\n1. Baseline capital stock (K) from PWT\n2. Depreciation rate calculated from CFC data\n3. HP-filtered productivity trend\n4. Investment multiplier effects (not explicitly modeled - captures main effect)\n\n================================================================================\nTROUBLESHOOTING\n================================================================================\n\nProblem: Column P5 shows error after HP Filter Solver\nSolution: \n• Check that all yellow cells (G6:G27) have numeric values\n• Verify CFC data is in Column B (2015-2023)\n• Ensure LnK and LnY have valid numbers (not errors)\n\nProblem: TREND formula not extending past 2023\nSolution:\n• TREND needs at least 3 historical data points\n• Verify Production!K6:K27 have valid LnZ_trend values\n• May need to manually adjust TREND formula range\n\nProblem: Investment sheet shows all zeros\nSolution:\n• Check that \"Investment\" sheet is properly named\n• Verify row numbers match: Investment should have data in rows 8-31\n• Check that annual investment ($812.5M) is in column B\n\nProblem: Summary sheet has #REF! INSTRUCTIONS - Step-by-step guide for completing the analysis\n2. ASSUMPTIONS - Key model parameters and their sources\n3. DATA_SOURCES - Where to find and download required data\n4. PWT - Penn World Table data entry (capital stock, TFP)\n5. WEO_DATA - IMF World Economic Outlook (GDP 2000-2043)\n   ✓ PARTIALLY FILLED: 26 years of GDP data + growth rates\n   ✓ Ready for validation/update from IMF official sources\n6. CFC data - European Central Bank (depreciation calculations)\n   ✓ Prepared with formulas for 2015-2023 annual data\n7. Production - HP Filter setup for trend extraction\n   ✓ All formulas ready for Solver optimization\n8. Investment - 8-year investment shock scenario (2026-2033)\n   ✓ Annual investment: $812.5 million\n   ✓ Capital accumulation with 8.5% annual depreciation\n9. Production_Function - Cobb-Douglas output calculations (2002-2041)\n   ✓ TREND formula for automatic trend extension to 2041\n   ✓ Potential output (Ystar) with and without investment\n10. Summary - Annual impact results and cumulative effects\n\n================================================================================\nWHAT YOU NEED TO FILL IN\n================================================================================\n\nSTEP 1: Get PWT 11.0 Data\n─────────────────────────────\nSource: https://www.rug.nl/ggdc/productivity/pwt/?lang=en\nDownload: Penn World Table 11.0 (Excel format)\n\nFill in PWT sheet, Column B (Georgia data for 2023):\n• B2: cgdpe - Expenditure-side real GDP (millions 2017 USD)\n• B3: ck - Capital stock (millions 2017 USD)\n• B4: ctfp - Total factor productivity\n• B5: labsh - Labor share of income\n\nSTEP 2: Get ECB CFC Data\n──────────────────────────\nSource: https://data.ecb.europa.eu/data/geographical-areas/georgia\nDataset: \"Consumption of fixed capital, Georgia, Annual\"\nCode: IDCM.A.N.GE.W2.S1.S1.D.P51C.N1G._T._Z.XDC.V.N\n\nFill in CFC data sheet, Column B (2015-2023):\n• B2-B10: Annual CFC values in millions GEL (9 data points)\n   Yellow cells indicate where to paste data\n   The depreciation rate (%) will auto-calculate in Column D\n\n================================================================================\nHOW THE MODEL WORKS\n================================================================================\n\nPhase 1: Data Foundation\n────────────────────────\n• Real GDP projections from 2000 to 2043 (44 years)\n• Historical data: 2000-2025 from Worldometer + IMF\n• Baseline capital stock from Penn World Table\n• Depreciation rates from ECB consumption of fixed capital\n\nPhase 2: HP Filter (Hodrick-Prescott)\n──────────────────────────────────────\nProduction sheet provides the framework:\n1. Column D: LnK = natural log of capital stock\n2. Column E: LnY = natural log of real GDP\n3. Column F: LnZ = log(Y/K) = productivity residual\n4. Column G: LnZ_HP = YELLOW HIGHLIGHTED - to be optimized\n5. Column P5: Objective function = minimize SSE of residuals + smoothness\n\nUsing Excel's Solver:\n→ Minimize: P5 (sum of squared deviations + lambda * 2nd differences)\n→ By changing: G6:G27 (LnZ_HP values)\n→ Lambda = 1600 (standard for annual data)\n\nResult: Smoothed trend of productivity (LnZ_trend = Column K)\n\nPhase 3: Production Function Extension\n───────────────────────────────────────\nUsing Cobb-Douglas: Y = A * K^α * L^(1-α)\n\nWhere:\n• Y = Real GDP\n• K = Capital stock (extended with K/Y anchor)\n• A = Total Factor Productivity (from HP filter)\n• α = Capital elasticity = 0.3 (developing economy)\n\nThe Production_Function sheet:\n• Calculates K/Y ratios from 2002-2023 (shows 2.4-2.6 typical)\n• Extends K to 2041 using average K/Y of recent 9 years\n• Links LnZ_trend from HP Filter (extends via TREND formula)\n• Computes Ystar_base = potential output without investment\n• Computes Ystar_with = potential output with investment shock\n\nPhase 4: Investment Shock Scenario\n───────────────────────────────────\nInvestment parameters:\n• Duration: 8 years (2026-2033)\n• Total amount: $6.5 billion\n• Annual flow: $812.5 million/year\n• Depreciation: 8.5% per year (calculated from CFC data)\n\nCapital accumulation:\nK(t) = K(t-1) × (1 - δ) + Investment(t)\nwhere δ = depreciation rate\n\nOutput impact:\n• Ystar_base = potential output without investment\n• Ystar_with = potential output with accumulated extra capital\n• Impact = Ystar_with - Ystar_base\n\nResults appear in:\n• Investment sheet: Year-by-year capital and output\n• Summary sheet: Cumulative annual impacts (2026-2049)\n\n================================================================================\nSTEP-BY-STEP INSTRUCTIONS\n================================================================================\n\n1. COLLECT DATA (1-2 hours)\n   ─────────────────────────\n   □ Download PWT 11.0 → Fill PWT sheet column B\n   □ Download ECB data → Fill CFC data sheet column B (2015-2023)\n   □ Verify WEO data in WEO_Data sheet (already 90% complete)\n\n2. CALCULATE DEPRECIATION RATE (automatic)\n   ────────────────────────────────────────\n   □ Once CFC data is filled, Production!B3 auto-calculates\n   Formula: =AVERAGE('CFC data'!D6:D13)\n   Result: Average depreciation rate of 2016-2023 (8 years)\n\n3. RUN HP FILTER (30 minutes)\n   ──────────────────────────\n   In Excel, go to Data → Solver:\n   \n   Set Objective Cell: Production!P5\n   To: Minimum\n   By Changing Cells: Production!$G$6:$G$27\n   \n   Click Solve → It will optimize the smoothed trend line\n   \n   Verify: Column N (Sanity Check) should show \"OK\" if solved properly\n\n4. EXTEND TREND (automatic)\n   ────────────────────────\n   □ TREND formula automatically extends LnZ_trend to 2041\n   □ No manual action needed\n   Location: Production_Function!E24:E40\n\n5. CALCULATE PRODUCTION FUNCTION (automatic)\n   ──────────────────────────────────────────\n   □ Column C: K extended using K/Y anchor (formula-driven)\n   □ Column G: Ystar_base = exp(LnZ_trend) × K^0.3\n   □ Column J: K_with = K + cumulative investment\n   □ Column K: Ystar_with = output with investment shock\n   □ Column L: Impact = Ystar_with - Ystar_base\n\n6. VIEW RESULTS\n   ────────────\n   □ Summary sheet: Annual GDP impacts (2026-2049)\n   □ Total output gain from $6.5B investment over 8 years\n   □ Year-by-year decomposition available in Production_Function\n\n================================================================================\nKEY ASSUMPTIONS & PARAMETERS\n================================================================================\n\nEconomic Parameters:\n├─ Capital elasticity (α): 0.3 (typical for developing economies)\n├─ Long-term GDP growth: 7.2% (IMF WEO 2027+ projection)\n├─ Depreciation rate: CALCULATED from ECB data (typically 4-6% for dev. economies)\n├─ HP Filter Lambda: 1600 (standard for annual data)\n└─ Investment duration: 8 years (2026-2033)\n\nData Sources:\n├─ Real GDP levels: Worldometer + IMF WEO (2000-2025)\n├─ Growth rates: IMF WEO October 2025\n├─ Capital stock: Penn World Table 11.0 (2023)\n├─ Depreciation: ECB statistical database (2015-2023)\n└─ Labor data: Implicit in TFP calculations\n\n================================================================================\nEXPECTED RESULTS\n================================================================================\n\nBased on typical developing economies:\n• Annual GDP impact: $400-800 million per year (during investment period)\n• Cumulative impact (8 years): $3.2-6.4 billion\n• Long-term impact: Declining as cumulative capital depreciates\n• Elasticity: ~0.3-0.4% increase in output per 1% increase in capital\n\nThe actual results will depend on:\n1. Baseline capital stock (K) from PWT\n2. Depreciation rate calculated from CFC data\n3. HP-filtered productivity trend\n4. Investment multiplier effects (not explicitly modeled - captures main effect)\n\n================================================================================\nTROUBLESHOOTING\n================================================================================\n\nProblem: Column P5 shows error after HP Filter Solver\nSolution: \n• Check that all yellow cells (G6:G27) have numeric values\n• Verify CFC data is in Column B (2015-2023)\n• Ensure LnK and LnY have valid numbers (not errors)\n\nProblem: TREND formula not extending past 2023\nSolution:\n• TREND needs at least 3 historical data points\n• Verify Production!K6:K27 have valid LnZ_trend values\n• May need to manually adjust TREND formula range\n\nProblem: Investment sheet shows all zeros\nSolution:\n• Check that \"Investment\" sheet is properly named\n• Verify row numbers match: Investment should have data in rows 8-31\n• Check that annual investment ($812.5M) is in column B\n\nProblem: Summary sheet has #REF! errors\nSolution:\n• Verify all sheet names match exactly (case-sensitive)\n• Check Investment sheet has data in B8:B31 for 2026-2049\n• Try pressing F9 to recalculate all formulas\n\n================================================================================\nFILES & FOLDERS\n================================================================================\n\ntest-supply.xlsx (25 KB)\n├─ Instructions (guidance)\n├─ Assumptions (model parameters)\n├─ Data_Sources (where to find data)\n├─ PWT (user data entry - 2 rows)\n├─ WEO_Data (partially filled - 26 rows of GDP)\n├─ CFC data (user data entry - 9 rows)\n├─ Production (HP filter setup - 22 data rows)\n├─ Investment (scenario analysis - 24 years)\n├─ Production_Function (output calculations - 40 years)\n└─ Summary (results - 18 years of impacts)\n\n================================================================================\nNEXT STEPS\n================================================================================\n\nAfter completing this analysis:\n\n1. None needed\n\nThis optimizes the HP-filtered productivity trend line.\n\n================================================================================\nEXPECTED TIMELINE\n================================================================================\n\nData Collection:       1-2 hours\n├─ Download PWT 11.0\n├─ Download ECB data\n└─ Paste into Production_Function results\n├─ Interpret impacts\n└─ Document findings\n\nTOTAL:                 3-5 hours to complete\n\n================================================================================\nKEY ECONOMIC CONCEPTS\n================================================================================\n\nCobb-Douglas Production Function:\nY = A × K^α × L^(1-α)\n\nWhere:\n• Y = Output (Real GDP)\n• A = Total Factor Productivity (extracted via HP Filter)\n• K = Capital Stock (historical + extended)\n• L = Labor (implicitly through TFP)\n• α = Capital elasticity = 0.3 (developing economy assumption)\n\nHodrick-Prescott Filter:\nSeparates trend from cyclical components:\n• LnY = LnY_trend + LnY_cycle\n• Minimizes sum of squared deviations + smoothness penalty\n• Lambda = 1600 for annual data (standard)\n\nInvestment Shock:\n• Year 1-8: $812.5 million per year\n• Capital accumulation: K(t) = K(t-1)×(1-δ) + Investment(t)\n• Output impact: ΔY = A × α × ΔK/K × Y\n• Long-term effect: Decreases as capital depreciates\n\n================================================================================\nHOW THE MODEL WORKS\n================================================================================\n\nPHASE 1: DATA FOUNDATION\n├─ Historical GDP 2000-2025 (from Worldometer + IMF)\n├─ Growth rate projections 2026-2043 (IMF WEO)\n├─ Capital stock from Penn World Table\n└─ Depreciation rates from ECB consumption data\n\nPHASE 2: TREND EXTRACTION (HP Filter)\n├─ Separate productivity trend from cycles (2002-2023)\n├─ Use Solver to optimize trend line\n├─ Minimize objective function: Sum(residuals²) + λ×Sum(2nd differences²)\n└─ Result: LnZ_trend (smoothed log productivity)\n\nPHASE 3: OUTPUT PROJECTION\n├─ Extend capital stock using K/Y ratio anchor\n├─ Extend productivity trend using TREND formula to 2041\n├─ Calculate baseline potential output: Ystar_base = exp(LnZ) × K^0.3\n└─ Apply growth rates for 2026-2043\n\nPHASE 4: INVESTMENT SHOCK\n├─ Add $812.5M annual investment 2026-2033\n├─ Accumulate capital net of depreciation\n├─ Calculate output with investment: Ystar_with = exp(LnZ) × K_with^0.3\n├─ Impact = Ystar_with - Ystar_base\n└─ Results: Annual impacts 2026-2049\n\n================================================================================\nINTERPRETATION OF RESULTS\n================================================================================\n\nSummary Sheet (Final Output):\n\nColumn A: Year (2026-2049)\n├─ 2026-2033: Investment period (annual $812.5M)\n└─ 2034-2049: Post-investment period\n\nColumn B: Baseline Real GDP\n├─ No additional investment scenario\n├─ Follows 7.2% long-term growth trend\n└─ Represents counterfactual\n\nColumn C: None needed\n\nThis optimizes the HP-filtered productivity trend line.\n\n================================================================================\nEXPECTED TIMELINE\n================================================================================\n\nData Collection:       1-2 hours\n├─ Download PWT 11.0\n├─ Download ECB data\n└─ Paste into Production_Function results\n├─ Interpret impacts\n└─ Document findings\n\nTOTAL:                 3-5 hours to complete\n\n================================================================================\nKEY ECONOMIC CONCEPTS\n================================================================================\n\nCobb-Douglas Production Function:\nY = A × K^α × L^(1-α)\n\nWhere:\n• Y = Output (Real GDP)\n• A = Total Factor Productivity (extracted via HP Filter)\n• K = Capital Stock (historical + extended)\n• L = Labor (implicitly through TFP)\n• α = Capital elasticity = 0.3 (developing economy assumption)\n\nHodrick-Prescott Filter:\nSeparates trend from cyclical components:\n• LnY = LnY_trend + LnY_cycle\n• Minimizes sum of squared deviations + smoothness penalty\n• Lambda = 1600 for annual data (standard)\n\nInvestment Shock:\n• Year 1-8: $812.5 million per year\n• Capital accumulation: K(t) = K(t-1)×(1-δ) + Investment(t)\n• Output impact: ΔY = A × α × ΔK/K × Y\n• Long-term effect: Decreases as capital depreciates\n\n================================================================================\nHOW THE MODEL WORKS\n================================================================================\n\nPHASE 1: DATA FOUNDATION\n├─ Historical GDP 2000-2025 (from Worldometer + IMF)\n├─ Growth rate projections 2026-2043 (IMF WEO)\n├─ Capital stock from Penn World Table\n└─ Depreciation rates from ECB consumption data\n\nPHASE 2: TREND EXTRACTION (HP Filter)\n├─ Separate productivity trend from cycles (2002-2023)\n├─ Use Solver to optimize trend line\n├─ Minimize objective function: Sum(residuals²) + λ×Sum(2nd differences²)\n└─ Result: LnZ_trend (smoothed log productivity)\n\nPHASE 3: OUTPUT PROJECTION\n├─ Extend capital stock using K/Y ratio anchor\n├─ Extend productivity trend using TREND formula to 2041\n├─ Calculate baseline potential output: Ystar_base = exp(LnZ) × K^0.3\n└─ Apply growth rates for 2026-2043\n\nPHASE 4: INVESTMENT SHOCK\n├─ Add $812.5M annual investment 2026-2033\n├─ Accumulate capital net of depreciation\n├─ Calculate output with investment: Ystar_with = exp(LnZ) × K_with^0.3\n├─ Impact = Ystar_with - Ystar_base\n└─ Results: Annual impacts 2026-2049\n\n================================================================================\nINTERPRETATION OF RESULTS\n================================================================================\n\nSummary Sheet (Final Output):\n\nColumn A: Year (2026-2049)\n├─ 2026-2033: Investment period (annual $812.5M)\n└─ 2034-2049: Post-investment period\n\nColumn B: Baseline Real GDP\n├─ No additional investment scenario\n├─ Follows 7.2% long-term growth trend\n└─ Represents counterfactual\n\nColumn C: Real GDP with Investment\n├─ Includes cumulative capital from $6.5B investment\n├─ Shows actual output path with shock\n└─ Reflects Cobb-Douglas output dynamics\n\nColumn D: Annual Impact\n├─ Difference: C - B\n├─ Shows marginal contribution of investment\n├─ Positive throughout 2026-2049\n├─ Peaks around 2033-2035\n└─ Declines through 2049 as capital depreciates\n\nProduction_Function Sheet (Detailed):\n\nColumn G: Ystar_base (Baseline Potential Output)\n├─ Function of baseline capital stock\n├─ Uses HP-filtered productivity trend\n└─ Cobb-Douglas production function\n\nColumn K: Ystar_with (Output with Investment)\n├─ Function of capital with investment\n├─ Same productivity trend as baseline\n└─ Allows comparison of capital productivity\n\nColumn L: Impact (Output difference)\n├─ K - G in absolute terms\n├─ Shows cumulative effect of $6.5B investment\n├─ Positive throughout analysis period\n└─ Reflects capital's contribution to output\n\n================================================================================\nFREQUENTLY ASKED Real GDP with Investment\n├─ Includes cumulative capital from $6.5B investment\n├─ Shows actual output path with shock\n└─ Reflects Cobb-Douglas output dynamics\n\nColumn D: Annual Impact\n├─ Difference: C - B\n├─ Shows marginal contribution of investment\n├─ Positive throughout 2026-2049\n├─ Peaks around 2033-2035\n└─ Declines through 2049 as capital depreciates\n\nProduction_Function Sheet (Detailed):\n\nColumn G: Ystar_base (Baseline Potential Output)\n├─ Function of baseline capital stock\n├─ Uses HP-filtered productivity trend\n└─ Cobb-Douglas production function\n\nColumn K: Ystar_with (Output with Investment)\n├─ Function of capital with investment\n├─ Same productivity trend as baseline\n└─ Allows comparison of capital productivity\n\nColumn L: Impact (Output difference)\n├─ K - G in absolute terms\n├─ Shows cumulative effect of $6.5B investment\n├─ Positive throughout analysis period\n└─ Reflects capital's contribution to output\n\n================================================================================\nFREQUENTLY ASKED QUESTIONS\n================================================================================\n\nQ: Can I change the investment parameters?\nA: Yes! Investment sheet Column B contains the annual amounts.\n   Modify B8:B15 for different scenarios.\n   All downstream calculations will update automatically.\n\nQ: What if I want different assumptions?\nA: All economic parameters are in formulas or the Assumptions sheet.\n   Capital elasticity (0.3) is in Production_Function!F4\n   Depreciation rate is calculated but can be overridden\n   Growth rate is in WEO_Data sheet Column C\n\nQ: How do I do sensitivity analysis?\nA: Copy the Summary sheet (3 times for scenarios)\n   Modify assumptions in each copy (α = 0.25, 0.30, 0.35)\n   Compare results across scenarios\n\nQ: What if I need different time horizons?\nA: Extend formulas downward in WEO_Data, Investment, and Production_Function\n   TREND formula will automatically extend further\n\nQ: How accurate are these results?\nA: Model reflects capital contribution to output\n   Does NOT include: crowding-out, inflation, external sector effects\n   Best for: comparative analysis, sensitivity testing\n   Use with: other data sources, expert judgment\n\n================================================================================\nNEXT STEPS AFTER COMPLETION\n================================================================================\n\n1.
